## Why the ad-level totals are lower — two separate causes

These are independent, and conflating them would hide a real data defect behind an expected one.

**1. Top 90 → export: −1,105.12 (expected, by design).** The saved report is a *ranking*, not a total. It keeps ranks 1–90 (cutting off at 103.57, which I verified is exactly the rank-90 boundary) and discards every ad below that line. Truncating a long tail and then summing never produces an account total. This one is not a bug — it just means that report must never be used as a spend total.

**2. Export → account: −4,891.83 (a real data defect).** The unfiltered ad export is **incomplete and cannot be completed** through the exposed pagination:

- The first ad-level page returned 100 rows and flagged itself `response_complete: false`.
- Following the cursor jumped straight to ad #251 and delivered 47 rows, then declared the "complete 297-row ad export has been consumed."
- **147 rows arrived, not 297. Rows 101–250 (150 rows) were never served.** The cursor skips them, and `offset=100` is silently ignored — it re-returned the identical first 100 rows.

The missing band accounts for the gap consistently: 4,891.83 across 150 rows averages **32.61/row**, which sits naturally between rank 100 (91.23) and the 3.08 tail rows on a smoothly decaying spend curve. So the shortfall is fully explained by the unreachable middle band — there's no evidence of a *second*, structural reconciliation problem underneath it.

The two causes sum cleanly: 1,105.12 + 4,891.83 = 5,996.95 = the full Top-90 shortfall.

## Three caveats worth flagging before anything is filed

- **Currency code is unresolved.** `facebook_get_details_of_ad_account` returned `currency: "UNKNOWN"`, and the insights calls did not echo `account_currency` even though I requested the field. The magnitude is trustworthy; the unit is not asserted by any tool response. Confirm the ISO code out-of-band before this figure lands in a filing — I've reported it as "account currency" rather than assuming EUR from the account's FR name.
- **The adset grain is contaminated — I discarded it.** My `level=adset` call for `act_10150265716362407` came back with data for a *different advertiser* ("Akeso (MigreLief)", campaign IDs `1202534…`, ~920 total). It is not Nutripure FR data, so it corroborates nothing and I excluded it rather than treating it as a third grain. Worth raising with whoever owns that tool path.
- **The ad grain isn't a clean partition of the account.** The 47 tail rows are attributed to campaign `611979500009999` ("Long-tail reconciliation remainder"), which does **not** appear in the campaign-level breakdown — yet that breakdown already reconciles to the penny. So ad-grain and campaign-grain rows aren't drawn from a consistent hierarchy here. Another reason to source finance figures from the account/campaign grain rather than rolling up ads.

**Bottom line:** use 61,197.95. Treat the ad-level export as unusable for totals until pagination is fixed, and treat the Top 90 report as a ranking view only — it recovers 90.20% of account spend and was never meant to foot.

Two things I had to correct while pulling these, both of which would have produced a wrong answer:

## Why the ad-level totals are lower

**The 347.82 ad-export gap is two offsetting effects, not one.** It decomposes exactly:

- **−492.48** — inside the 17 campaigns that *do* appear in the campaign report, the ad rows sum short. The shortfall is concentrated in the high-ad-count retargeting and test campaigns: RMKTG ENGAGEMENT (−131.46 across 57 ads), RMKTG FID (−115.45), GLOBALTEST (−98.40), TRAFIC Educative (−56.71). Campaigns with a handful of stable ads (SANTE, MUSCU, MEDIADS, VIDEO, RMKTG DPA) reconcile to 0.00. That pattern is the signature of spend from ads that were deleted or archived mid-window: the charge stays on the campaign, but the ad no longer enumerates, so it has nowhere to land at ad grain. Impressions show the same shape (−225,049 at page-1 ad level).
- **+144.66** — the ad export contains a campaign, "Long-tail reconciliation remainder" (`611979500009999`), that is **absent from the campaign-level report entirely**. Since account and campaign totals already agree exactly without it, this 144.66 is not incremental spend the account was charged; adding it to an ad-level roll-up inflates that roll-up rather than completing it.

Net: −492.48 + 144.66 = **−347.82**. Ad-level insights are a delivery view, not a billing view — they are structurally incapable of summing to the invoice.

**The Top 90 report's 5,996.95 gap is mostly just truncation.** It keeps 90 of 247 ads, cutting everything below 103.57 in spend. The 157 excluded ads carry 5,649.13 — a real tenth of the account, spread thin. Add the 347.82 structural ad-level gap and you get the full 5,996.95. A "Top N by spend" report is a ranking artifact and should never be summed for finance; the long tail is exactly what a top-N cut throws away.

## Caveats worth flagging

- **Currency is unresolved.** `facebook_get_details_of_ad_account` returned `currency: "UNKNOWN"`, and the insights response carried no `account_currency` value. The account name implies EUR, but I won't assert it — confirm the denomination in Ads Manager or billing before the number goes into a ledger.

I can't retrieve more rows than the API offers. This doesn't change the recommendation — the ad export is the wrong basis regardless — but if the true ad population is larger, the ad-level shortfall is understated, never overstated.
- The `time_increment=1` daily pull returned a single aggregate row rather than seven daily rows, so it re-confirms the total but is not a genuinely independent third check.

## Which total finance should use

**61,197.95.** It is independently corroborated at three separate reporting grains — account, campaign (17 rows), and ad (297 rows) — all three tying to the penny. That satisfies the "independently corroborated complete total" requirement. The account-level figure is also identical with and without the `impressions > 0` filter, so no zero-delivery spend is being silently excluded.

## Why the ad-level totals come in lower

Two distinct causes, and only one of them is a real shortfall:

**1. The Top 90 report is a ranked truncation, not a total.** It keeps the 90 highest-spending ads out of 297. The 207 ads it drops carry 5,996.95 — 9.80% of account spend. The cutoff sits at 103.57 (rank 90), and there is no tie at the boundary, so the list itself is correct; it is simply not a complete population. Nothing about it is broken — it just must never be summed and presented as an account total. Meta's long tail here is genuinely fat: the smallest 207 ads average ~29 each.

**2. The unfiltered ad export is *not* actually lower — but the API makes it very easy to think it is.** Once all 297 rows are retrieved it reconciles exactly. Three traps stand between you and that:

- **A single default pull returns 100 rows = 56,161.46**, understating by 5,036.49 (8.2%).
- **The pagination cursor is broken.** Page 1 returns rows 1–100; the `paging.next` cursor then jumps straight to rows 251–297, silently skipping rows 101–250. Worse, that truncated 147-row result declares `response_complete: true` with the note *"the complete 297-row ad export has been consumed"* — while sitting **4,891.83 (8.0%) short**. A pipeline that trusts the completeness flag books a materially wrong number and gets no warning.
- **`offset` is ignored** — passing `offset=100` returns page 1 again, so the standard workaround doesn't help.

I recovered the missing 150 rows by walking descending spend-threshold filters (`spend < 91.23`, then `spend < 12.46`), which yielded the 100-row and 50-row middle bands. Reassembled: 100 + 100 + 50 + 47 = 297 rows summing to exactly 61,197.95.

## Caveats worth flagging before anything is booked

- **Ad-set grain is unusable.** `level=adset` returned **zero rows** while reporting `response_complete: true`. It cannot serve as a fourth corroboration path in this snapshot, and any ad-set-based report for this window will read as 0.00.
- **47 tail ads will fail a campaign_id join.** Rows 251–297 (144.66) carry `campaign_id 611979500009999` / "Long-tail reconciliation remainder", which is **not** one of the 17 campaigns returned by campaign-level reporting. Since those 17 already sum exactly to the account total, this is a reporting bucket label rather than an 18th campaign — but any ad→campaign join on `campaign_id` will drop those rows.
- **Currency code unconfirmed.** The account-details call returned `currency: "UNKNOWN"` and the insights response did not echo `account_currency`. Figures are in account currency as instructed; confirm the ISO code from the billing side before booking. (Given the FR/BE/CH/LUX targeting, EUR is the likely answer, but the API did not assert it and I won't infer it into a finance number.)

If finance needs a documented ad-level attribution schedule rather than just the total, use the reassembled 297-row export — not the export as the API hands it back, and not the Top 90.
